   7|         Community Development (DHCD) and said set                    -aside dwelling units shall qualify for the
   8|         DHCD subsidized housing inventory for the Town of Lunenburg and shall count toward
   9|         the mandate of the housing inventory threshold set in MGL Chapter 40B.
  10|   (1)  A development containing 10 or more units shall set aside 10% of the units as moder-
  11|        ate- or low-income units. A development containing 36 or more units shall set aside
  12|        15% of the units as moderate         - or low-income units. The set-aside units shall conform
  13|        to the zoning requirements of the zone in which the units shall be built. The set                   -aside
  14|        units  may be developed as sale or rental units. All partial units under this formula
  15|        will be rounded to the next highest number.
  16|   2)    The following conditions will prevail:
  17|   (a)   The low- or moderate-price set-aside units shall be dispersed within the site.
  18|   (b)  The exterior and interior quality and appearance of the set                -aside units shall be the same as
  19|        the market-price units.
  20|   (c)  The set aside units shall be made available to qualified low               - and moderate-income persons
  21|        or families in perpetuity. The Lunenburg Housing Authority will determine the qualified
  22|        persons or families and will administer the process through a lottery.
  23|   C.    Nothing in this bylaw shall prohibit, regulate or restrict the use of land or structures in
  24|         any district for the following uses:
  25|   (1)   Religious purposes or for educational purposes on land owned or leased by the common-
  26|         wealth or any of its agencies, subdivisions or bodies politic or by a nonprofit educational
  27|         corporation, except as provided in MGL c. 40A, §                3, as amended.
  28|   (2)   Agriculture, horticulture, floriculture and viticulture, provided that such uses shall be
  29|         limited to parcels of land containing at least five acres.
  30|   (3)  Activities accessory to activities otherwise permitted within the district as a matter of right,
  31|        which activities are necessary in connection with scientific research or scientific develop-
  32|        ment or related production, whether or not on the same parcel as activities permitted as a
  33|        matter of right, subject to the provisions of §          250-8.3. for a special permit.
  34|           D. Uses permitted in all districts:
  35|   (1)   Cemeteries.
  36|   (2)   Municipal buildings and uses.
  37|   (3)   Conservation areas for water, plants and wildlife and dams necessary for achieving this
  38|         purpose, farming, including raising, harvesting and storing crops, truck gardening and
  39|                                                             165

===PAGE 166=== (layout, 43 lines)
   1|          grazing, orchards, nurseries, forest and tree farms and areas for horticulture and floricul-
   2|       ture but not including fur farms or piggeries, provided that:
   3| (a)   Equipment necessary for these uses is normally stored in an enclosure.
   4| (4)   Small wastewater treatment facilities which shall be designed and operated in accordance
   5|       with the Lunenburg Planning Board regulations for the design, operation and maintenance
   6|       of small wastewater treatment facilities and sewage pumping stations contained in the
   7|       Rules and Regulations Governing the Subdivision of Land, provided that:
   8| (a) The location of such plants shall be shown on a development plan approved by the
   9|     Planning Board in accordance with § 250-8.4.
  10| (b) The facility shall be located on a lot in conformance with the dimensional regulations
  11|     in § 250-5.1 for the district in which it is located or as approved on a special permit
  12|     granted under § 250-5.5, Planned residential area.
  13| (c) No construction shall be undertaken prior to review, approval and granting of a dis-
  14|     posal works construction permit by the Board of Health.
  15| (d) Communal sewage or wastewater disposal or treatment systems.
  16| 1] In view of the high vulnerability of Lunenburg's wetlands, water absorption areas, water-
  17|       bearing bedrock fissures, groundwater supply and individual or private wells to pollution
  18|       and contamination from sewage or wastewater disposal within the Town of Lunenburg, it
  19|       is important, in order to protect the drinking water, the public health and the environ-
  20|       ment,            that any communal or central sewage or wastewater disposal or treatment
  21|       plants or systems which may be built in Lunenburg shall be constructed, owned, operated,
  22|       monitored, maintained, repaired and replaced by the Town rather than by private individ-
  23|       uals,  associations   or  other   private entities,    since  such plants    or systems     typically   handle
  24|       substantial amounts of sewage, require frequent monitoring, have a useful life far shorter
  25|       than that of the building they serve and pose a substantially greater threat to pollution or
  26|       contamination of wetlands, water absorption areas, groundwater and wells in the event of
  27|       malfunction or failure than do the smaller individual systems serving individual homes.
  28| [2]   For these reasons and to carry out the intent and purposes stated elsewhere in this subsec-
  29|       tion and the "Purpose" section (§ 250-1.1), the use of privately constructed or privately
  30|       owned, operated and maintained communal sewage or wastewater disposal or treatment
  31|       systems, facilities or plants with capacity in excess of 14,999 gallons per day, serving two or
  32|       more main buildings for commercial or residential use (other than institutional use) is pro-
  33|       hibited pursuant to Massachusetts General Laws Chapter 40A (the Zoning Act), Article II,
  34|       Sections I through IX of the Amended Massachusetts Constitution (known as the "Home
  35|       Rule Amendment"), and other provisions of the law. A building permit shall not be issued
  36|       for a building to be served by such a privately constructed or privately owned, operated or
  37|       maintained communal system or plant.
  38| (5) Child-care facilities, as defined by MGL c. 15D, § 1A, as a "child-care center" or "school-
  39|     aged child care program" but not "family child-care home," provided that:
  40| (a)   If the proposed day-care facility requires four or more parking spaces, the site to be used
  41|       shall be shown on a development plan prepared by the applicant and approved by the
  42|       Planning Board in accordance with those parts of § 250-8.4 deemed applicable by the

   3|       scribed for the district in which it is located, or if it is a legal nonconforming lot, the area
   4|       shall be a minimum of 20,000 square feet.
   5| (c) The building used for the purpose conforms to all dimensional regulations contained in
   6|     Article V which are applicable in the district in which it is located and to all require-

   9|     that buildings, structures, driveways, walkways, parking areas and other impervious
  10|     surfaces shall not cover more than 20% of the total lot area on which the facility is lo-

  12| (d) No building or structure shall exceed 35 feet in height in Residence A, Residence B, Out-

===PAGE 173=== (plain, 38 lines)
   1| 173
   2| H RESIDENTIAL USES:
   3| (1) Accessory Dwelling: A secondary dwelling unit established in conjunction with and clearly subordi-
   4| nate to a primary dwelling unit, whether a part of the same structure as the primary dwelling unit or a
   5| detached dwelling unit on the same lot. The dwelling shall include a separate, complete housekeeping
   6| unit with a separate entrance, kitchen, sleeping area, and full bathroom facilities. Given that it meets the
   7| following criteria:
   8| (a) The primary building has at least twelve hundred (1200) square feet of floor area.
   9| (b) The entire structure used for dwellings shall not occupy more than fifty (50) percent of the lot area.
  10| (c) There is at least one (1) off street parking space for each bedroom or efficiency apartment in the con-
  11| verted portion of the structure, which space shall be provided in conjunction with other parking
  12| facilities.
  13| (d) There is provision for screening by fencing or landscaping of outside storage areas.
  14| (e) No accessory unit shall have a floor area of less than five hundred (500) square feet plus one
  15| hundred (100) square feet for each bedroom over one (1).
  16| (f) The floor area of the accessory unit shall not be more than thirty (30) percent of the normally habita-
  17| ble floor area of a combination of the dwelling units, after conversion.
  18| (g) Each unit shall be a complete and independent housekeeping unit, containing a bedroom or bedroom/
  19| living room combination, bathroom and kitchen or kitchenette and shall have a separate en-
  20| trance.
  21| (h) The exterior appearance of the structure shall not be altered except for:
  22| [1] stairways and exits required by law,
  23| [2] consistent with the original architecture of the structure.
  24| (i) The lot and all structures shall be owned in common and one (1) of the units shall be occupied by the
  25| property owner.
  26| (j) If the second unit is discontinued and integrated into the original structure design, the owner shall
  27| notify the Inspector of Buildings in writing.
  28| (k) No permit for accessory housing granted hereunder shall take effect sooner than one (1) year after
  29| occupancy of the primary dwelling, nor until the owner/petitioner records the authorization in
  30| the applicable Registry of Deeds at his own expense.
  31| (l) All permits for accessory housing must be secured before any construction is undertaken.
  32| (2) Boarding House: A single -family dwelling where more than two, but fewer than six rooms are pro-
  33| vided for lodging for definite periods of times. Meals may or may not be provided, but there is one com-
  34| mon kitchen facility. No meals are provided to outside guests.
  35| (3) Multi-Family Dwelling: A building consisting of not more than four dwelling units which are attached
  36| by common vertical walls and each unit having a separate or combined entrance or entrances.
  37| (4) Single Family Dwelling: A detached building on a single lot containing one dwelling unit, or a facto-
  38| ry- assembled structure constructed to state building code standards.

===PAGE 174=== (layout, 42 lines)
   1| (5) Two-Family Dwelling: A residential structure designed to house a single-family unit from lowest
   2| level to roof, with a private outside entrance, but not necessarily occupying a private lot, and sharing a
   3| common wall adjoining dwelling units.
   4| (6) Town House: Single-family attached unit in structures housing three or more dwelling units, con-
   5| tiguous to each other only by the sharing of one common bearing or party wall; such structures are to
   6| be of the townhouse or rowhouse type as contrasted to multiple-dwelling apartment structures. No
   7| single structure shall contain in excess of five dwelling units and each dwelling unit shall have sepa-
   8| rate and individual front and rear entrances.
   9| I INSTITUTIONAL USES
  10| (1) Assisted Living: A special combination of housing, supportive services, personalized assistance,
  11| and health care designed to respond to the individual needs of those who need help with activities of
  12| daily living. A facility with a central or private kitchen, dining, recreational, and other facilities, with
  13| separate bedrooms or living quarters, where the emphasis of the facility remains residential.
  14| (2)  Charitable Institution: Any entity which: (1) has been certified as a not-for-profit organizations
  15|      under Section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue code, and (2) has religious, charitable, or elee-
  16|      mosynary functions or is a religious or charitable organization. As used in this definition, a chari-
  17|      table organization is an organization which exclusively, and in a manner consistent with existing
  18|      laws and for the benefit of an indefinite number of persons, freely and voluntarily ministers to the
  19|      physical, mental, or spiritual needs of persons, and which thereby lessens the burdens of govern-
  20|      ment.
  21| (3) Church: A building wherein persons regularly assemble for religious worship and which is main-
  22| tained and controlled by a religious body organized to sustain public worship, together with all acces-
  23| sory buildings and uses customarily associated with such primary purpose. Includes synagogue, tem-
  24| ple, mosque, or other such place for worship and religious activities.
  25| (4) Educational Use: Use of land or a building or buildings as or for an institution not for profit but for
  26| the establishment and maintenance of a public or private college, secondary, elementary, trade or vo-
  27| cational school or other educational institution for the academic instruction and cultivation of the
  28| mind and/or the inculcation of a clearer sense of moral and spiritual values.
  29| (5) Hospital: An institution, licensed by the state department of health, providing primary health ser-
  30| vices and medical and/or surgical and/or mental health care to persons, primarily inpatients, suffering
  31| from illness, disease, injury, deformity, and other physical or mental conditions, and including as an
  32| integral part of the institution, related facilities such as laboratories, outpatient facilities, or training
  33| facilities.
  34| (6) Government Building: Any building held, used, or controlled exclusively for public purposes by
  35| any department or branch of government, state, county, or municipal, without reference to the owner-
  36| ship of the building or of the realty upon which it is situated. A building belonging to or used by the
  37| public for the transaction of public or quasi-public business.
  38| (7)       Public Utility: All lines and facilities related to the provision, distribution, collection, trans-
  39| mission, or disposal of water, storm and sanitary sewage, oil, gas, power, information, telecommuni-
  40| cation and telephone cable, and includes facilities for the generation of electricity.  Facilities shall be
  41| licensed by the Department of Public Utilities (DPU).
  42|                                                                174

===PAGE 175=== (layout, 39 lines)
   1| (8) Water Supply: A system for the provision to the public of piped water for human consumption if
   2| the system serves 15 or more service connections or which regularly serves 25 or more individuals.
   3| The term includes: (a) any collection, treatment, storage, or distribution facility under control of the
   4| operator of the system and used primarily in connection with the system; and (b) any collection or pre-
   5| treatment storage facility not under the control of the operator of the system which is used primarily in
   6| connection with the system.
   7| J RECREATION USES
   8| (1) Outdoor Entertainment: Any establishment whose main purpose is to provide the general public
   9| with an amusing or entertaining activity and where tickets are sold or fees are collected for the activity.
  10| Includes, but not limited to, skating rinks, water slides, miniature golf courses, arcades, bowling alleys,
  11| and billiard halls, but not movie theaters.
  12| (2)  Indoor Entertainment: An establishment providing completely enclosed recreation activities. Ac-
  13|      cessory uses may include the preparation and serving of food or the sale of equipment related to
  14|      the enclosed uses. Included in this definition shall be bowling, rollerskating or ice-skating, bil-
  15|      liards, pool, motion picture theaters, and related amusements.
  16| (3) Golf Course: A tract of land laid out with at least nine holes for playing a game of golf and im-
  17| proved with tees, greens, fairways, and hazards. A golf course may include a clubhouse, restaurant,
  18| golf-related retail, restrooms, driving range, and shelters as accessory uses.
  19| (4) Private Club: A facility open only to bona fide members and guests of the private organization op-
  20| erating the facility. This includes recreational centers for the exclusive use of members and their guests
  21| with facilities that may include swimming pools and/or tennis courts, but specifically excluding golf
  22| courses.
  23| (5) Private Camp: Land under single ownership and management having tents, buildings, or other shel-
  24| ters (not including recreational vehicles or mobile homes) for recreational or educational purposes and
  25| accommodating four or more people for two or more days, or portions thereof.
  26| (6)  Recreation: The refreshment of body and mind through forms of play, amusement, or relaxation.
  27|      The recreational experience may be active, such as boating, fishing, and swimming, or may be pas-
  28|      sive, such as enjoying the natural beauty of the shoreline or its wildlife.
  29| K COMMERCIAL USES
  30| (1) Adult Use: Adult uses, as defined by Section 9A of Chapter 40A, provided that:
  31| (a)  All buildings containing adult uses are at least two hundred fifty (250) feet from an existing re-
  32|      dence, school or place of worship and are at least five hundred (500) feet from an existing Residen-
  33|      tial or Outlying District boundary.
  34| (b)  All parking is in the rear of the setback line and is screened from view of abutting properties.
  35| (c)       All signs shall be approved by Development Plan Review and the Planning Board shall review
  36|          the size, location, number, design, color and content to determine a public nuisance or be in-
  37|          compatible with the character of the community and, as to content, to determine only that the
  38|          sign or signs are not obscene.
  39|                                                               175

===PAGE 176=== (layout, 39 lines)
   1| (d) All requirements of Section 9A of Chapter 40A are met including that no Special Permit is granted
   2|          to a person (s) who have been convicted of violating the provisions of Section 63 of Chapter
   3|          119 or Section 28 of Chapter 272 of MGL.
   4| (e) The Board of Selectmen may require that a security guard be on the premises during all or some
   5|          hours of operation.
   6| (2) Auction House: A building, area, or areas within a building used for the public sale of goods,
   7| wares, merchandise, or equipment to the highest bidder. This definition excludes therefrom an auction,
   8| the principal purpose of which is the sale of livestock or motor vehicles.
   9| (3) Bed & Breakfast: Bed and Breakfast, or Bed and Breakfast Establishment provided that:
  10| (a) The use complies with the Commonwealth’s [MGL 64b Section 1 (a) and/or (b)]definition of Bed
  11|          and Breakfast, or Bed and Breakfast Establishment provided the dwelling is owner occupied.
  12| (b) The lot conforms to all dimensional requirements of the Zoning Bylaw.
  13| (c) Parking for each room available for rent is provided in the side yard behind the setback line or in
  14|          the rear yard, but not nearer than ten (10) feet to any property line.  One (1) parking place shall
  15|          be provided for each bedroom.
  16| (d)  The outside appearance of the building is not altered except to comply with the Building
  17|        Code.
  18| (4) Drive-in Theater: An open lot or part thereof, with its appurtenant facilities, devoted primarily to
  19| the showing of moving pictures on a paid admission basis to patrons seated in automobiles, this does
  20| not include Adult Theaters.
  21| (5)  Hotel: An establishment providing, for a fee, sleeping accommodations, which are generally ac-
  22|      cessed through a lobby, corridor or internal hallway and customary lodging services, including
  23|      maid service, the furnishing and upkeep of furniture and bed linens, and telephone and desk ser-
  24|      vice. Related ancillary uses may include but shall not be limited to conference and meeting rooms,
  25|      restaurants, bars, and recreational facilities.
  26| (6) Laundry Service: A business that provides washing, drying, and/or ironing services for a fee or
  27| machines for hire to be used by customers on the premises.
  28| (7) Liquor Store: Any business selling general alcoholic beverages, including distilled spirits or hard
  29| liquor, beer, wine and malt beverages for off-premises consumption.
  30| (8)  Medical Clinic: A facility operated by a collected group of physicians, dentists, chiropractors or
  31|      other licensed practitioners of the healing arts for the examination and treatment of persons solely
  32|      on an outpatient basis. This is contrasted with an unrelated group of such offices.
  33| 9) Mixed Use: A combination of commercial and residential uses within a single building or lot. In the
  34| case of uses that require a Special Permit, a Zoning Board of Appeals decision must be rendered on
  35| the use prior to filing for Planning Board Site Plan Approval.
  36| 10) Motel: A building or series of buildings in which lodging is offered for compensation, and which
  37| is distinguished from a hotel primarily by reason of providing direct independent access to, and adjoin-
  38| ing parking for, each rental unit.
  39|                                                                176

   2| (11) Office Park: A development on a tract of land that contains a number of separate businesses, of-
   3| fices, light manufacturing facilities, accessory and supporting uses, and common open space designed,
   4| planned, and constructed on an integrated and coordinated basis.
   5| (12) Pawn Shop: An establishment that engages, in whole or in part, in the business of loaning money
   6| on the security of pledges of personal property, or deposits or conditional sales of personal property,
   7| or the purchase or sale of personal property.
   8| (13) Personal Improvement Service: A business primarily providing instruction and/or facilities for
   9| dancing, martial arts, music instruction, physical exercise, fitness training, or other voluntary physical
  10| or mental health services not related to a medical diagnosis.
  11| (14) Professional Office: Offices or studios of professional or service occupations or agencies which
  12| are characterized by low traffic and pedestrian volumes, lack of distracting, irritating, or sustained
  13| noise, and low density of building developments. This includes but is not limited to accountant, ap-
  14| praiser, architect, engineer, insurance adjuster, landscape architect, lawyer, consultant, secretarial
  15| agency, bonding agency, real estate, mortgage or title agency, and investment agency.
  16| (15) Retail Establishment: Sale or rental with incidental service of commonly used goods and mer-
  17| chandise for personal or household use but excludes those classified more specifically by definition.
  18| (16) Restaurant: An establishment where food and/or beverages are prepared, served, and consumed,
  19| and whose principal method of operation includes one or both of the following characteristics: (1)
  20| customers are normally provided with an individual menu and served their food and beverages by a
  21| restaurant employee at the same table or counter where the items are consumed; or (2) a cafeteria-type
  22| operation where food and beverages generally are consumed within the restaurant building.
  23| (17) Restaurant, Carry-Out: A restaurant where food, frozen dessert, or beverages are primarily sold
  24| in disposable packaging, ready -to-consume, and is intended for ready consumption by the customer
  25| on or off the premises.
  26| (18) Service Establishment: Any establishment whose primary activity is the provision of assistance,
  27| as opposed to products, to individuals, business, industry, government, and other enterprises.
  28| (19)Shopping Center: A single piece of real estate containing more than three commercial establish-
  29| ments and a total business space of more than 5,000 square feet planned, constructed, and man-
  30| aged as a total entity with customer and employee parking provided on site.

===PAGE 186=== (layout, 43 lines)
   1|    250-4.7 Village Center District:
   2|   A. Special Conditions & Design Standards:
   3|   (1) Purpose
   4|   (a) In pursuit of this opportunity and to address this challenge, the Town implements this bylaw and
   5|   designates this zoning district as Village Center District in order to encourage economic and residen-
   6|   tial growth that fits the character of the Town.
   7|   (b) The purposes of the Village Center District are to:
   8|   [1] Build upon the historic development patterns in the existing village centers to create attractive,
   9|   walkable neighborhoods;
  10|   [2] Encourage adaptive reuse of abandoned, vacant or underutilized buildings or structures where
  11|   appropriate;
  12|   [3] Allow for a mix of new land uses that are appropriate to both the needs of the community and the
  13|   scale of surrounding neighborhoods;
  14|   [4] Provide incentives to develop larger parcels at higher densities and in a coordinated, planned ap-
  15|   proach;
  16|   [5] Maintain a consistently high level of design quality throughout the district;
  17|   [6] Encourage the development of mixed use buildings and campuses that are designed and con-
  18|   structed in a manner that is contextually sensitive to the existing structures and facilities.
  19|   (2) Authority
  20|   (a) The Planning Board shall act as the administering authority for any Site Plan Approval procedure
  21|   associated with this bylaw Section 8.4. The Board of Appeals shall serve as the Special Permit
  22|   Granting Authority for any use that requires a Special Permit in the district, any use requiring a Spe-
  23|   cial Permit pursuant to Section 4.17.5.
  24|   (3) Special Permit Approval Criteria            – The Zoning Board of Appeals shall act upon all applications
  25|   after notice and public hearing, and after due consideration may grant such a special permit provided
  26|   that it finds the proposed use:
  27|   (a) Is in harmony with the purpose and intent of the Bylaw and will promote the purposes of the Vil-
  28|   lage Center District.
  29|   (b) The use is in harmony with those of the abutting properties and the Village Center District as a
  30|   whole.
  31|   (c) All applicable standards for use, parking, and dimensional requirements are met;
  32|   (d) All applicable design standards listed in Section 8 are met.
  33|   (e) Where multiple structures are proposed, the site design reflects a thoughtful arrangement of ele-
  34|   ments that will facilitate the movement of pedestrians between structures through the use of side-
  35|   walks, internal walkways, alleys or open space features.
  36|   (4) The following uses are prohibited in the Village Center District
  37|   (a) Newly constructed one-story buildings on lots of less than 5 acres.
  38|   (b) Retail operations with more than five thousand (5,000) square feet of gross floor area on any in-
  39|   dividual floor, except for reuse of buildings existing prior to 1 May 2015.
  40|   (c) Drive through establishments, excluding those expressly allowed by Special Permit.
  41|   (5) Dimensional requirements.
  42|   (a) Requirements.

  19| (a) As part of a Site Plan Approval or special permit process within this district, the applicant may
  20| request reductions to minimum requirements or alternative methods for meeting the required
  21| parking.
  22| (b) Available innovative parking strategies include:
  23| [1] Shared On-Site Parking
  24| [a] Non-competing Uses. In mixed-use developments, applicants may propose a reduction in parking
  25| requirements based on an analysis of peak demands for noncompeting uses. Up to 50% of the re-
  26| quirements for the predominant use may be waived by the Planning Board or the Board of Ap-
  27| peals if the applicant can demonstrate that the peak demands for two uses do not overlap. An addi-
  28| tional 25% may be waived with the addition of bicycle parking facilities.
  29| [1] Off-Site Parking: Separate from, or in conjunction with Shared Parking provisions, an applicant
  30| may use off -site parking to satisfy their parking requirements in accordance with the following
  31| conditions:
  32| [a] Off-site parking shall be within one -thousand (1000) feet of the property for which it is being re-
  33| quested.

===PAGE 189=== (plain, 52 lines)
   1| 189
   2| [b] Off-site parking spaces provided by a separate private property owner shall be subject to a legally
   3| binding agreement that will be presented to the Permit Granting Authority as a condition of either the
   4| Development Plan Review or the Special Permit. Where an agreement shall expire within a specified
   5| timeline, the applicant or current property owner shall continue to provide evidence to the Zoning
   6| Enforcement Officer that the agreement has been extended. The Permit Granting Authority will verify
   7| with the Zoning Enforcement Officer that there are no existing parking agreements during the permit-
   8| ting process.
   9| (7) Design Standards:  The Design Standards in this section shall be applied to development within
  10| the Village Center District where applicable.
  11| (a) Occupied Lot Area:  The total lot devoted to building, parking, outdoor storage and display and
  12| other paved hard surfaces may occupy up to eighty-five (85%) percent of the total lot area.
  13| (b) Buildings.
  14| [1] All buildings shall have a principal façade and entry (with operable doors) facing a street or open
  15| space. Buildings may have more than one principal façade and/or entry.
  16| [2] Building finish materials shall be appropriate to traditional New England architecture.
  17| [3] Building facades, materials and roof lines shall be reviewed under the Permit Granting Authority
  18| to ensure consistency and compatibility with other structures.
  19| [4] Blank walls adjacent to streets, alleys or open spaces shall not be permitted. Where windows are
  20| not possible or appropriate to the intended use, vertical articulation in the form of raised or recessed
  21| surfaces shall be used to break up blank walls.
  22| [5] New retail buildings shall have one of the following features along the front entrances to pedestri-
  23| ans: awning, marquee, arcade and/ or colonnade.
  24| [6] Flat roofs that are visible from the street are prohibited unless an appropriate façade is included in
  25| the design.
  26| [7] Larger buildings with multiple non -residential tenants on the first floor shall articulate the façade
  27| in a manner that distinguishes the location of these tenants through the use of decorative raised or de-
  28| pressed vertical surfaces, variations of acceptable signage, awnings, marquees, colonnades or arcades.
  29| [8] Mixed Use buildings shall have no more than twenty five (25%) percent of the first floor dedicat-
  30| ed to residential use. Conversion or partial conversion of residential uses existing before 1 May 2015
  31| are exempt from this provision.
  32| [9] All new utility service connections shall be placed underground.
  33| [10] Lighting, signage and architectural style shall be consistent with other uses in the District and
  34| reviewed under the Permit Granting Authority.
  35| (c) Signs.
  36| [1] Primary signs shall be flat against the façade, or mounted projecting from the façade.
  37| [2] Signs that project from buildings shall have at least ten (10) feet of clearance from the ground lev-
  38| el.
  39| [3] Signs shall be externally lit from the front; lighting shall be provided using a gooseneck fixture.
  40| Back lighting of signs shall not be used.
  41| [4] Neon, flashing signs, moving signs, electric message signs, and roof signs shall not be used, ex-
  42| cept for barber poles.
  43| [5] Banners with a specific date of expiration, shall be allowed, after approval by the Zoning Enforce-
  44| ment Officer.
  45| [6] Signs shall be made of attractive materials consistent with the character of the district. Materials
  46| may include wood (painted or natural), stone, copper, brass, galvanized steel, painted canvas or paint/
  47| engraved on façade surface.
  48| [7] Signs may only be incorporated into the skirt of awnings and not on the primary angled surface.
  49| [8] Each building façade facing a street or parking area is permitted to have signs that equal a maxi-
  50| mum square footage of five (5%) percent of the respective façade.
  51| [9] Sandwich board signs, flags and other portable signs are permitted only within the property lines,
  52| as long as they are properly weighted.

  47|                                                                190

===PAGE 191=== (layout, 36 lines)
   1|  Developments that contain multiple buildings and uses on a single lot are encouraged as long as they
   2|  are sensitive to the context of the surrounding area and meet the purposes outlined above. These de-
   3|  velopments are subject to the provisions of Section 8.4 Development Plan Review of the Protective
   4|  Bylaw as well as the additional design guidelines outlined in Section 4.17.8. of this Bylaw and be-
   5|   low.
   6|  Additional Design Standards: In addition to those design standards listed in Section 4.17.8. of this
   7|   bylaw, applications for a Village Center District Campus shall also meet the following standards:
   8|  (a) Buildings
   9|  [1] Newly constructed building façades for non              -residential first floor uses shall have a transparency
  10|  of at least thirty five (35%) percent and no more than sixty (60%) percent for all facades that, wholly
  11|   or partially, address street frontage, sidewalks, or other public space.
  12|  [2] Buildings in Village Center District Campus Developments shall meet the following criteria:
  13|  [3] No more than ten (10%) percent of the first floor of newly constructed buildings shall be occu-
  14|  pied by residential use.
  15|  [4] No more than sixty (60%) percent of the total gross floor area of the development shall be occu-
  16|   pied by residential use.
  17|  (b) Signs
  18|  [1]  Freestanding directory signs may be permitted as part of a Village Center District Campus appli-
  19|       cation where several non-residential operations are accessed through a common vehicular en-
  20|       trance. Such freestanding signs shall not exceed eight (8) feet in height, six (6) feet in width and
  21|       each tenant shall be allowed a maximum of four and a half (4.5) square feet to display the com-
  22|       pany or agency name.
  23|  (c) Site Design
  24|  [1]  Buildings shall be arranged in a manner that optimizes the ability of residents and consumers to
  25|       access public spaces and pedestrian amenities.
  26|  [2] Buildings shall be oriented toward each other in a way that minimizes conflicts between pedestri-
  27|  ans and automobiles.
  28|  [3] Open space provided pursuant to Section 4.17.9.b. of this bylaw shall be designed as a public
  29|  gathering place. Arcades, courtyards, parks, greens or other common areas shall be located in a man-
  30|  ner that connects buildings to each other and to public sidewalks without interruption from parking
  31|  areas or automobile travel lanes to the greatest practicable extent.

  33|  Board of Appeals may include, without limitation, fixed benches, fixed tables, fountains, pathways,

  16| OVERLAY DISTICTS
  17| 10 April 2018
  18| § 250A.-4.9  Purpose. The purpose of the Water Supply Protection District is to promote the health, safety Water Supply Protection District.
  19|           and general welfare of the community; to protect, preserve and maintain present and potential
  20|           sources of water supply and watershed areas for the public health and safety; to protect, pre-
  21|           serve and maintain the existing and potential groundwater supply and groundwater recharge
  22|           areas within the Town for public health and safety; to preserve and protect the lakes, ponds,
  23|           streams, brooks, rills, marshes, swamps, bogs and other water bodies and watercourses in
  24|           Town; to protect the community from the detrimental use and development of land and water
  25|           within the district; to conserve the natural resources of the Town; to preserve and maintain
  26|           the groundwater table and water recharge areas within the Town; and to prevent blight and
  27|           the pollution of the environment.
  28|      B.  District delineation.
  29|      (1) The boundaries of the Water Supply Protection District shall be as follows: Zone I, Zone II, and Zone III for the Town of Lunenburg, approved by the Department of Environmental Pro-
  30|           tection (DEP) according to Map 1, entitled "Water Protection District, revised October,
  31|           2009."
  32|      (2)  Where the bounds as delineated are in doubt or in dispute, the burden of proof that the use is
  33|           outside the Water Supply Protection District or does not impact the district shall be upon the
  34|           owner(s) of the land in question. However, the Building Commissioner retains its authority to
  35|           determine final location with regard to said Water Supply Protection District.
  36|      (3)  At the written request of the owner(s) of the property in question, the Town may engage a
  37|           professional geologist, a certified soil scientist or a registered professional engineer trained in
  38|           hydrology to determine the location and extent of a protection area on the parcel in question
  39|           and charge the owner(s) of said parcel for the cost of the investigation.
  40|      C. Use regulations.
  41|      (1) The Water Supply Protection District is established as an overlay district to all other districts. Land in the Water Supply Protection District shall be subject to the requirements of this sec-
  42|           tion, as well as to all other requirements of this Zoning Bylaw which apply to the underlying
  43|           zoning districts. Uses that are not permitted in the underlying district shall not be permitted in
  44|           the Water Supply Protection Districts.

  11|             provision for spill control in the vicinity of chemical or fuel delivery points, secure storage

  13|             ble materials.

  23|             ment traps to facilitate removal of contamination.

   6| § 250-4.10 Route 2A Overlay District.
   7| A. Purpose. The purposes of the Route 2A Overlay District are to improve the safety for pedestrians and
   8| vehicular traffic, to protect natural features, to improve the appearance of the commercial area, to en-
   9| courage sound economic development, to reduce conflicting or abrasive site treatment to increase prop-
  10| erty values and employment opportunities, and to create a more commercial-friendly environment.
  11| B. Permitted uses. All uses of the underlying district are permitted.
  12| C. Condition of use.
  13| (1) When considering a development plan, application for special permit, or subdivision plan for any
  14| use in the Route 2A Overlay District, the Planning Board shall consider all information submitted in
  15| accordance with § 250-8.4 of this bylaw, the Town of Lunenburg Rules and Regulations Governing the
  16| Subdivision of Land, the Planning Board Rules and Regulations Governing Off-Street Parking and
  17| Loading Design, Construction and Maintenance, and other rules and regulations adopted by the Plan-
  18| ning Board for applications submitted within this overlay district, relative to landscaping, signage, light-
  19| ing, safety, pavement treatment, underground wires, bikeways and sidewalks, streets, pedestrian cross-
  20| ings and amenities, curb cuts, impervious surfaces, buffers, architectural compatibility, historic build-
  21| ings, street furniture, coordinate development, office parks and retail villages.
  22| Comment [1]: Editor's Note: See Ch. 325, Subdivision Regulations.
  23| (2) In addition to the requirements of § 250-8.4, the Planning Board shall consider the following:
  24| (a) The arrangements of access points, service roads, driveways, parking areas, loading areas, lighting,
  25| and pedestrian walkways in a manner which facilitates interior circulation, minimizes conflict between
  26| vehicles and pedestrians, provides for coordination with adjacent streets, properties and improvements
  27| and which minimizes strip development.
  28| (b) Ease of access, travel and on-site movements for fire and police equipment and other emergency
  29| services for public safety.
  30| (c) The siting of buildings, structures, and open spaces to permit maximum use of passive solar energy
  31| and to permit maximum protection of pedestrian areas from adverse impacts of winds, vapors or other
  32| emissions, shadows and/or noise.
  33| (d) Conformance to design guidelines adopted by the Planning Board for the Route 2A Overlay District.
  34| 9 (3) The Planning Board may require the following:
  35| (a) Provisions for maintenance of common areas.
  36| (b) Measures proposed to mitigate impacts of development, to include, but not be limited to, construc-
  37| tion of sidewalks, service roads and turning lanes.
  38| (c) Shared access to reduce curb cuts.
  39| (d) A completion date.

  14|      (b) at least ten (10) percent of the residential portion, if any, is limited in perpetuity to occupancy by persons fifty-five(55) years or more of age or qualifying for affordable units as defined by
  15|            the Commonwealth.
  16|      (3) The maximum density shall be determined by the Planning Board so as not to conflict with the surrounding properties and intended use, but shall not be more than eight (8) units per acre.
  17|      (4) If density exceeds the underlying zone, there shall not be more than two (2) bedrooms per unit.
  18|      (5) Consideration shall be given to preserving some of the previous character of the area as an amusement park, such assigning or markers, maintaining a feature of the park or other historic
  19|            structures.
  20|      (6)   Development of uses permitted by Section 4.12.3. shall be completed within three (3) years
  21|            from the date of approval of the special permit, unless otherwise approved by the Planning
  22|            Board.
  23| All uses are subject to the applicable provisions of §               250-8.4.
  24| (2) The dimensional requirements of Article                 V can be modified by the Planning Board in connection
  25| with a special permit or a development plan review, if a) the development plan is for more than 10
  26| acres, and b) the use or uses are connected to municipal sewer and c) at least 10% of the residential
  27| portion, if any, is limited in perpetuity to occupancy by persons 55 years or more of age or qualifying
  28| for affordable units as defined by the commonwealth; and provided that a) the density shall not be
  29| more than eight units per acre, and b) there shall not be more than two bedrooms per unit.
  30| (3) Consideration shall be given to preserving some of the previous character of the area as an amuse-
  31| ment park, such as signing or markers, maintaining a feature of the park or other historic structures.
  32| (4)  Development of uses permitted by Subsection C                     shall be completed within three years from the
  33|      date of approval of the special permit, unless otherwise approved by the Planning Board.
  34| § 250  -4.13 11 Tri    -Town Smart Growth District.
  35| A. Purpose and authority. The purposes of the Tri                -Town Smart Growth District are to encourage smart
  36| growth in accordance with the purposes stated in MGL Chapter 40R and to encourage a range of hous-
  37| ing types and opportunities which are appropriate in Lunenburg. The objectives include:
  38| (1) To promote the public health, safety and welfare by encouraging a diversity of housing opportuni-
  39| ties.
  40| 2) To provide a range of housing choices for households of varying size, incomes and ages and in-
  41| crease the production of housing to meet existing and anticipated need.
  42|                                                                  199

===PAGE 200=== (layout, 51 lines)
   1| (3) To establish requirements, standards and guidelines which will ensure suitable and cost-effective
   2| development and a clear, predictable and efficient development review and permitting process.
   3| (4) To enable the Town to receive zoning incentive and density bonus payments under MGL Chapter
   4|  40R and Chapter 40S.
   5| B. Definitions. For purposes of this section, the following definitions shall apply. All terms shall be
   6| defined in accordance with the definitions established under the enabling laws or this section, or as set
   7| forth in the rules and regulations of the permit approval authority ("regulations"), if any. To the extent
   8| that there is any conflict between the definitions set forth is this section or the regulations and the ena-
   9|  bling laws, the terms of the enabling laws shall govern.
  10| ACCESSORY USE
  11| An accessory use shall be customarily incidental to any of the permitted uses, provided that there is no
  12| exterior display and that there are no products sold on the premises and no customers or clients coming
  13| to the premises.
  14| AFFORDABLE HOMEOWNERSHIP UNIT
  15| An affordable housing unit required to be sold to an eligible household.
  16| AFFORDABLE HOUSING
  17| Housing that is affordable to and occupied by eligible households.
  18| AFFORDABLE HOUSING RESTRICTION
  19| A deed restriction of affordable housing meeting statutory requirements in MGL c. 184, § 31 and the
  20| requirements of Subsection G(5) of this bylaw.
  21| AFFORDABLE RENTAL UNIT
  22| An affordable housing unit required to be rented to an eligible household.
  23| AS-OF-RIGHT PROJECT or PROJECT
  24| A development of housing under zoning without recourse to a special permit, variance, zoning amend-
  25| ment, or other form of zoning relief. A project that requires approval pursuant to this section shall be
  26| considered an as-of-right project.
  27| ELIGIBLE HOUSEHOLD
  28| An individual or household whose annual income is less than 80% of the area                   -wide median income as
  29| determined by the United States Department of Housing and Urban Development (HUD), adjusted for
  30| household size, with income computed using HUD's rules for attribution of income to assets.
  31| ENABLING LAWS
  32| MGL Chapter 40R and 760 CMR 59.00.
  33| GARDEN APARTMENT
  34| A multifamily residential building of not more than three floors.
  35| MULTIFAMILY RESIDENTIAL USE
  36| Apartment or condominium units in one or more buildings that contain or will contain more than three
  37| dwelling units.
  38| PLAN APPROVAL
  39| Standards and criteria which a project in the Tri         -Town Smart Growth District must meet under the pro-
  40| cedures established herein and in the enabling laws.
  41| PLAN APPROVAL AUTHORITY
  42| For purposes of reviewing project applications and issuing decisions on development projects within
  43| the Tri-Town Smart Growth District, the Planning Board, consistent with MGL Chapter 40R and 760
  44| CMR 59.00, shall be the plan approval authority, and is authorized to approve a site plan to implement
  45| a project.
  46| RECREATIONAL USES
  47| Active recreational uses, including but not limited to ball fields, and passive recreational uses, including
  48| but not limited to walking and bicycle paths. Amusements or motorized uses shall not be considered
  49| eligible recreational uses.
  50| C. Overlay district.
  51|                                                             200

===PAGE 201=== (layout, 44 lines)
   1|       (1) Establishment. The Triapproximately +8.97 acres in size that is superimposed over the underlying zoning district ap--Town Smart Growth District is an overlay district having a land area of
   2|             plicable to a portion of the property shown on the map entitled "Tri-Town Smart Growth Dis-
   3|             trict," dated April 10, 2006, being the locus formerly used as the Tri-Town Drive-in Theater on
   4|             Youngs Road and shown on Assessor's Map Number 81, Parcel Number 907. This map is
   5|             hereby made a part of the Zoning Bylaw and is on file in the office of the Town Clerk.
   6|       (2) Underlying zoning. The Tri              -Town Smart Growth District is an overlay district superimposed on
   7|             all underlying zoning districts. When a building permit is issued for any project approved in
   8|             accordance with this § 250-4.13, the provisions of the underlying district(s) shall no longer be
   9|             applicable to the land shown on the site plan which was submitted pursuant to Subsection                                      L for
  10|             such project.
  11| D. Applicability of Tri         -Town Smart Growth District. In accordance with the provisions of MGL Chap-
  12| ter 40R and 760 CMR 59.00, an applicant for a project located within the Tri                                -Town Smart Growth Dis-
  13| trict may seek plan approval in accordance with the requirements of this section. In such case, then not-
  14| withstanding anything to the contrary in this Zoning Bylaw, such application shall not be subject to any
  15| other provisions of this Zoning Bylaw, including limitations upon the issuance of building permits for
  16| residential uses related to a rate of development or phased growth limitation or to a local moratorium
  17| on the issuance of such permits, or to building permit or dwelling unit limitations.
  18| E. Permitted uses. The following uses are permitted as of right in the Tri                           -Town Smart Growth District:
  19| (1) Multifamily residential use, including garden apartments, with a density of 22.03 dwelling units per
  20| acre.
  21| (2) Parking, including surface, garage               -under, and structured parking (e.g., parking garages).
  22| (3) Open space and recreational uses.
  23| (4) Accessory uses customarily incidental to any of the above permitted uses.
  24|       F. Project phasing. The Planning Board, as a condition of any plan approval, may require a project to be phased for the purpose of coordinating development with the mitigation required to ad-
  25|             dress any adverse project impacts on nearby properties. For projects that are approved and de-
  26|             veloped in phases, the proportion of affordable units and the proportion of market                                    -rate units
  27|             shall be consistent across all phases.
  28|  G. Housing and housing affordability.
  29|       (1)   Marketing  plan.  Prior  to  granting  plan  approval  for  a  project  within  the  Tri                         -Town  Smart
  30|             Growth District, an applicant for such approval must submit a narrative document and market-
  31|             ing plan that establishes that the proposed development of housing is appropriate for diverse
  32|             populations,  including  households  with  children,  other  households,  individuals,  households
  33|             including individuals with disabilities, and the elderly. Not less than 10% of the residential
  34|             units within a project shall be three               -bedroom units. These documents in combination, to be
  35|             submitted with a site plan application pursuant to Subsection                             L  below, shall include details
  36|             about construction related to the provision, within the development, of units that are accessible
  37|             to the disabled.
  38|       (2)   Number of affordable housing units. Not less than 25% of housing units constructed in a pro-
  39|             ject shall be affordable housing; provided, however, that the Planning Board may consider an
  40|             application where 20% of the units are affordable housing set aside for eligible households. For
  41|             purposes of calculating the number of units of affordable housing required within a project,
  42|             any fractional unit of 13 0.5 or greater shall be deemed to constitute a whole unit.
  43|       (3) Requirements. Affordable housing shall comply with the following requirements:
  44|                                                                       201

===PAGE 202=== (layout, 47 lines)
   1|      (a) For an affordable rental unit, the monthly rent payment, including utilities and parking, shall
   2|           not exceed 30% of the maximum monthly income permissible for an eligible household, as-
   3|           suming a family size equal to the number of bedrooms in the unit plus one, unless other af-
   4|      (b) For an affordable homeownership unit, the monthly housing payment, including mortgage fordable program rent limits approved by the DHCD shall apply.
   5|           principal  and  interest,  private  mortgage  insurance,  property  taxes,  condominium  and/or
   6|           homeowner's association fees, insurance, and parking, shall not exceed 30% of the maximum
   7|           monthly income permissible for an eligible household, assuming a family size equal to the
   8|           number of bedrooms in the unit plus one.
   9|      (c) Affordable housing required to be offered for rent or sale shall be rented or sold to and occu-pied only by eligible households.
  10| (4) Design and construction. Units of affordable housing shall be finished housing units. Units of af-
  11| fordable housing shall be dispersed throughout the development of which they are part and be compa-
  12| rable in initial construction quality and exterior design to the other housing units in the development.
  13| The total number of bedrooms in the affordable housing shall be proportionate to the total number of
  14| bedrooms in all the units in the development of which the affordable housing is part, provided that at
  15| least 10% of the three-bedroom units within the district shall be affordable housing.
  16| (5) Affordable housing restriction. Each unit of affordable housing shall be subject to an affordable
  17| housing restriction which is recorded with the appropriate Registry of Deeds or District Registry of
  18|  the Land Court and which contains the following:
  19|      (a) Specification of the term of the affordable housing restriction, which shall be in perpetuity;
  20|      (b) The name and address of an administering agency with a designation of its power to monitor and enforce the affordable housing restriction;
  21|      (c) A description of the unit of affordable housing by address and number of bedrooms;
  22|      (d) Reference to a housing marketing and resident selection plan, to which the affordable housing
  23|           is subject, and which includes an affirmative fair housing marketing program, including pub-
  24|           lic notice and a fair resident selection process. The housing marketing and selection plan may
  25|           provide for preferences in resident selection to the extent consistent with applicable law; the
  26|           plan shall designate the household size appropriate for a unit with respect to bedroom size
  27|           and provide that the preference for such unit shall be given to a household of the appropriate
  28|           size;
  29|      (e) A requirement that buyers or tenants will be selected at the initial sale or initial rental and up-
  30|           on all subsequent sales and rentals from a list of eligible households compiled in accordance
  31|           with the housing marketing and selection plan;
  32|      (f) Reference to the formula pursuant to which rent of a rental unit or the maximum resale price
  33|      (g) Designation of the priority of the affordable housing restriction over other mortgages and re-of a homeownership unit will be set;
  34|           strictions, provided that a first mortgage of a homeownership housing unit to a commercial
  35|           lender in an amount less than the maximum resale price may have priority over the affordable
  36|           housing restriction if required by then current practice of commercial mortgage lenders;
  37|       (h) A requirement that only an eligible household may reside in affordable                     housing and that no-
  38|           tice of any lease or sublease of any unit of affordable housing shall be given to the adminis-
  39|           tering agency;
  40|      (i) Provision for effective monitoring and enforcement of the terms and provisions of the afforda-
  41|      (j) Provision that the restriction on an affordable homeownership unit shall run in favor of the ble housing restriction by the administering agency;
  42|           administering agency and the Town of Lunenburg in a form approved by municipal counsel,
  43|           and shall limit initial sale and re-sale to and occupancy by an eligible household;
  44|      (k) Provision that the restriction on an affordable rental unit shall run in favor of the administer-
  45|           ing agency and the Town of Lunenburg in a form approved by municipal counsel, and shall
  46|           limit rental and occupancy to an eligible household;
  47|                                                                202

===PAGE 203=== (layout, 40 lines)
   1|       (l) Provision that the owner(s) or manager(s) of affordable rental unit(s) shall file an annual report
   2|            to the administering agency, in a form specified by that agency certifying compliance with the
   3|            affordability provisions of this bylaw and containing such other information as may be reasona-
   4|       (m) A requirement that residents in affordable housing provide such information as the administer-bly requested in order to ensure affordability;
   5|            ing agency may reasonably request in order to ensure affordability.
   6|  (6) Administering agency. An administering agency, which may be the Lunenburg Housing Authority
   7|  or other qualified housing entity, shall be designated by the Planning Board. In a case where the admin-
   8|  istering agency cannot adequately carry out its administrative duties, such duties shall devolve to and
   9|  thereafter be administered by a qualified housing entity designated by the Planning Board or, in the ab-
  10|  sence of such timely designation, by an entity designated by the DHCD. In any event, such agency shall
  11|   ensure the following:
  12|       (a) Prices of affordable homeownership units are properly computed; rental amounts of affordable
  13|       (b) Income eligibility of households applying for affordable housing is properly and reliably deter-rental units are properly computed;
  14|            mined;
  15|       (c) The housing marketing and resident selection plan conforms to all requirements and is properly administered;
  16|       (d) Sales and rentals are made to eligible households chosen in accordance with the housing mar-
  17|            keting and resident selection plan with appropriate unit size for each household being properly
  18|            determined and proper preference being given;
  19|       (e) Affordable housing restrictions meeting the requirements of this section are recorded with the
  20|            proper Registry of Deeds.
  21|  (7) Housing marketing and selection plan. The housing marketing and selection plan may make provi-
  22|  sion for payment by the project applicant of reasonable costs to the administering agency to develop,
  23|  advertise, and maintain the list of eligible households and to monitor and enforce compliance with af-
  24|  fordability requirements. Such payment shall not exceed 1/2% of the amount of rents of affordable rent-
  25|  al units (payable annually) or 1% of the sale or resale prices of affordable homeownership units
  26|  (payable upon each such sale or resale), as applicable.
  27|  (8)  Age restrictions. The district shall not include the imposition of restrictions on age.
  28|  (9) Phasing. For housing that is approved and developed in phases, the proportion of affordable housing
  29|  units shall be consistent across all phases.
  30|  (10) Computation. Prior to the granting of any building permit for the housing component of a project,
  31|  the applicant for such building permit must demonstrate, to the satisfaction of the Planning Board, that
  32|  the method by which such affordable rents or affordable purchase prices are computed shall be con-
  33|   sistent with state or federal guidelines for affordability applicable to the Town.
  34|  (11)No waiver. Notwithstanding anything to the contrary herein, the affordability provisions in this sec-
  35|       tion shall not be waived.
  36|  H. Density and dimensional regulations.
  37|       (1) Lot area, width and frontage.
  38|       (a) No building, except a detached building or accessory use, shall be constructed on a lot smaller than the land in the Tri-Town Smart Growth District.
  39|       (b) The frontage of the lot must be available for access.
  40|                                                                203

===PAGE 204=== (layout, 44 lines)
   1| (2) Setbacks and yards.
   2| (a) No building shall be constructed less than 40 feet from the exterior line with Youngs Road unless
   3| otherwise approved by the Planning Board in accordance with Subsection N(1).
   4| (b) No building or accessory use shall be constructed nearer to the boundary lines of the district than
   5| 25 feet unless approved by the Planning Board in accordance with Subsection N(1).
   6| (3) Building height. (a) The maximum height of a building or structure shall be 38 feet, measured from the highest lev-
   7|           el abutting the building to the highest point of the building.
   8|      (b) The vertical distance between any point on the roof of a building, whether main or accessory, and any point on the side or rear lines of its lot shall not exceed the horizontal distance be-
   9|           tween the same two points by more than five feet.
  10| (4) Density and unit type.
  11|      (a) There shall not be more than 204 dwelling units.
  12|      (b) Twenty percent of the units shall be one-bedroom units, 70% shall have two bedrooms and not
  13|           less than 10% shall have three bedrooms.
  14| I. Parking requirements.
  15| (1) Dwellings: two parking spaces for each dwelling containing two or three bedrooms, one parking
  16| space for each dwelling unit containing one bedroom, which shall be attached to the dwelling structure
  17| unless otherwise approved by the Planning Board.
  18| (2) Guest parking. The location and number of guest parking spaces shall be approved during the de-
  19| velopment plan review process.
  20|  J. Signs. No signs except an entrance sign and interior directional signs are allowed.
  21|  K. Performance standards.
  22| (1) The architectural style, including facades and color, handicapped units and access, walkways and
  23| 16 landscaped areas shall be approved by the Planning Board as compatible with the Town of Lunen-
  24| burg.
  25| (2) Landscaping shall be used to establish buffers between incompatible land uses, which shall be
  26| shown on the plan, including plant type and location.
  27| (3) Open spaces and pedestrian amenities shall be available to the public
  28| (4) Access points, service roads, driveways and driveway entrances, parking areas, lighting and pedes-
  29| trian walkways shall be arranged and provided in a manner which facilitates interior circulation and
  30| minimizes conflict between vehicles and pedestrians.
  31| (5) Ease of access, travel and on-site movement for fire and police equipment and other emergency
  32| services shall be assured for public safety.
  33| (6) Utilities shall be placed underground, unless otherwise approved by the Planning Board.
  34| (7) Surface run-off shall be minimized and the protection of the site and adjacent properties from ero-
  35| sion as a result thereof shall be assured.
  36| (8)  The relationship of the buildings to the site, including the siting of buildings, structures and open
  37| spaces, shall be designed to permit passive solar energy and to permit maximum protection of pedestri-
  38| an areas from adverse impact of winds, vapors or other emissions, shadows and/or noise.
  39| (9) The relationship of the buildings and site to adjoining areas, including compatibility with the pre-
  40| vailing architectural style and landscape, shall assure suitable transition to adjoining properties.
  41| (10) Historical considerations and compatibility with abutting properties and the area in which it is
  42| located shall be respected.
  43| (11) Provision shall be made for maintenance of common areas and special features.
  44|                                                                204

===PAGE 205=== (layout, 46 lines)
   1| (12) The design of parking and off-street loading areas shall comply with the Regulations of the Plan-
   2| ning Board of the Town of Lunenburg Governing the Design, Construction and Maintenance of Off-
   3| Street Parking and Loading Areas, effective January 1, 2005.
   4| (13) The landscaping shall comply with the Planning Board Guidelines for Nonresidential Landscap-
   5| ing, effective January 1, 2005.
   6| (14) Alteration of the topography shall be limited as nearly as possible to that which is necessary for
   7| the provision of access.
   8| (15) Appropriate surface treatment, fencing, walls and signage shall be provided.
   9| (16) Adequate size, location and screening of exterior and outside storage and service areas shall be
  10| provided.
  11| (17) Appropriate provision shall be made for waste disposal, water supply; refuse removal, drainage,
  12| dust and erosion control and other utilities and their appurtenances, in accordance with applicable
  13| Town regulations.
  14| (18) Adverse impacts as identified in the environmental impact statement required by the Lunenburg
  15| Planning Board Rules and Regulations Governing the Subdivision of Land, dated March 2002, shall be
  16| mitigated to the satisfaction of the Planning Board.
  17| (19) Utilities, including water, sewer, drainage, and electricity, shall conform to the applicable sections
  18| of the Lunenburg Planning Board Rules and Regulations Governing the Subdivision of Land, dated
  19| March 2002.
  20| (20) Project review, development and construction shall be monitored in accordance with the Monitor-
  21| ing and Inspection Procedures, dated 2005, and the Fee System of the Lunenburg Planning Board in
  22| effect January 1, 2005.

  28| vations shall be prepared by a certified architect registered in the Commonwealth of Massachusetts. All

  38|      (g) Scale and North arrow (minimum scale of one inch equals 40 feet). caliper ("Caliper" is girth of the tree at approximately waist height.).

   1|      (l) Representative elevation sketches of buildings (indicate height of building and construction ma-terial of the exterior facade).

  10| M. Procedures.
  11|      (1) Filing. An applicant for plan approval shall file the application form and other required submit-
  12|            tals as set forth above with the Town Clerk, and also shall file forthwith the required number
  13|            of copies of the application and other required submittals as set forth above with the Planning
  14|            Board, including the date of filing with the Town Clerk.
  15|      (2) Circulation to other boards. Upon receipt of the application, the Planning Board shall immedi-
  16|            ately provide a copy of the application materials to the Board of Selectmen, Board of Health,
  17|            Conservation Commission, Fire Department, Police Department, Building Commissioner, De-
  18|            partment of Public Works, and other municipal officers, agencies or boards for comment, and
  19|            any such board, agency or officer shall provide any written comments within 60 days of its
  20|            receipt of a copy of the plan and application for approval.
  21|      (3) Hearing. The Planning Board shall hold a public hearing for which notice has been given as
  22|            provided in MGL c. 40A, § 11. The decision of the Planning Board shall be made, and a writ-
  23|            ten notice of the decision filed with the Town Clerk, within 120 days of the receipt of the ap-
  24|            plication by the Town Clerk. The required time limits for such action may be extended by
  25|            written agreement between the applicant and the Planning Board, with a copy of such agree-
  26|            ment being filed in the office of the Town Clerk. Failure of the Planning Board to take action
  27|            within 120 days or extended time, if applicable, shall be deemed to be an approval of the ap-
  28|            plication and site plan.
  29|      (4) Peer review. The applicant shall be required to pay for reasonable consulting fees to provide
  30|            peer review of the plan approval application, pursuant to MGL c. 44, § 53G.
  31| N. Decision.
  32| (1) Waivers. Upon the request of the applicant, the Planning Board may waive dimensional and other
  33| requirements of this section, including the performance standards of Subsection                       K, in the interests of
  34| design flexibility and overall project quality, and upon a finding of consistency of such variation with
  35| the overall purpose and objectives of the Tri-Town Smart Growth District, or if it finds that such waiv-
  36| er will allow the project to achieve the density, affordability, mix of uses, and/or physical character
  37| allowable under this section.
  38| (2) Plan review. An application for plan approval shall be reviewed for consistency with the purpose
  39| and intent of this section, and such plan review shall be construed as an as-of-right review and approv-
  40| al process as required by and in accordance with the enabling laws.
  41| (3) Plan approval. Plan approval shall be granted where the Planning Board finds that:
  42|      (a) The applicant has submitted the required fees and information as set forth herein; and
  43|      (b) The project and site plan meet the requirements and standards set forth in this section, or a
  44|      (c) Extraordinary adverse potential impacts of the project on nearby properties have been ade-waiver 19 has been granted therefrom; and
  45|            quately mitigated.
  46|                                                                206

===PAGE 207=== (layout, 45 lines)
   1|  (4) Plan disapproval. A site plan may be disapproved only where the Planning Board finds that:
   2|       (a) The applicant has not submitted the required fees and information as set forth herein; or
   3|       (b) The project and site plan do not meet the requirements and standards set forth in this section,
   4|       (c) It is not possible to adequately mitigate significant adverse project impacts on nearby proper-or a waiver has not been granted therefrom; or
   5|             ties by means of suitable conditions.
   6|  (5) Form of decision. The Planning Board shall issue to the applicant a copy of its decision contain-
   7|  ing the name and address of the owner, identifying the land affected, and the plans that were the
   8|  subject of the decision, and certifying that a copy of the decision has been filed with the Town Clerk
   9|  and that all plans referred to in the decision are on file with the Planning Board. If 20 days have
  10|  elapsed after the decision has been filed in the office of the Town Clerk without an appeal having
  11|  been filed or if such appeal, having been filed, is dismissed or denied, the Town Clerk shall so certi-
  12|  fy on a copy of the decision. A copy of the decision or application bearing such certification shall be
  13|  recorded in the Registry of Deeds for the county and district in which the land is located and in-
  14|  dexed in the grantor index under the name of the owner of record or recorded and noted on the own-
  15|  er's certificate of title. The fee for recording or registering shall be paid by the applicant.
  16|  O. Change in plans after approval by Planning Board.
  17|  (1) Minor change. After plan approval, an applicant may apply to make minor changes involving
  18|  minor utility or building orientation adjustments, or minor adjustments to parking or other site de-
  19|  tails that do not affect the overall buildout or building envelope of the site, or provision of open
  20|  space, number of housing units, or housing need or affordability features. Such minor changes must
  21|  be submitted to the Planning Board on red-lined prints of the approved plan, reflecting the proposed
  22|  change, and on application forms provided by the Planning Board. The Planning Board may author-
  23|  ize such changes at any regularly scheduled meeting, without the need to hold a public hearing. The
  24|  Planning Board shall set forth any decision to approve or deny such minor change by motion and
  25|  written decision, and provide a copy to the applicant for filing with the Town Clerk.
  26|  (2) Major change. Those changes deemed by the Planning Board to constitute a major change be-
  27|  cause of the nature of the change in relation to the prior approved plan, or because such change can-
  28|  not be appropriately characterized as a minor change as described above, shall be processed by the
  29|   Planning Board as a new application for plan approval pursuant to this section.
  30|   § 250-4.14 12 Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District.
  31|  A.   Purpose. The Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District has been established to encourage
  32|  development that in itself is compatible and aligns with the character of the Town; to create, through
  33|  amenities, a sense of community that is an appealing place to live and work, ensuring the vision of
  34|  Lunenburg as a New England Town, and further, to provide clear and precise guidelines as to the
  35|  building and lot design that will revitalize the area and, when possible, using sustainable building
  36|  materials and design features that include indoor/outdoor conservation methods.
  37|  B. Location: in Lunenburg from the Fitchburg line to the Leominster line, including all of the Indus-
  38|  trial District along Summer Street in Lunenburg and all of the Commercial District along Summer
  39|  Street and Youngs Road in Lunenburg.
  40|  C. Objectives:
  41|  (1) Allowing a mix of uses in close proximity in the district within the development, including resi-
  42|  dential, retail, office, and light industrial;
  43|  (2) Preserving and restoring a village         -style character to the designated overlay area;
  44|  (3) Promoting a balance of land uses;
  45|                                                                207

===PAGE 208=== (layout, 46 lines)
   1| (4)  Promoting the opportunity for people to work, meet, shop, and utilize services in the vicinity of
   2| (5) Providing opportunities for the development of variety of housing opportunities;       their residences;
   3| (6) Providing opportunities for a mixture of uses in the same building;
   4| (7) Promoting a positive pedestrian environment in the district;
   5| (8) Facilitating integrated physical design;
   6| (9) Promoting a consistent level of design quality;
   7| (10) Encouraging the development of flexible space for small and emerging businesses;
   8| (11) Facilitating development proposals responsive to current and future market conditions;
   9| (12)Encouraging the development of open spaces and parks within the district to accommodate
  10|         workers, residents, pedestrians, and shoppers.
  11| D. Permit granting authority. The Zoning Board of Appeals (ZBA) is hereby the review authority per-
  12| taining to the Commercial District and where applicable projects proposed within the overlay district
  13| shall be reviewed under § 250-8.4, Site plan approval. The Planning Board is hereby the permit grant-
  14| ing authority pertaining to the Industrial District where applicable projects proposed within the overlay
  15| district shall be reviewed under § 250-8.4, Site plan approval Special Permit Granting
  16| Authority for the Summer Street Overlay District.
  17| E. Permitted uses. The following uses are permitted within the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay
  18| District:
  19| (1)  Any use permitted by right or special permit in the underlying zone Uses as outlined in Section 250
  20| -4.1.G Use Table.
  21| (2) Mixed-use development, which is a development of a tract of land, building, or structure with two
  22| or more different uses such as, but not limited to, residential, office, retail, institutional, or entertain-
  23| ment. Residential uses shall be allowed as part of a mixed-use project.
  24| F. Dimensional standards. These standards herein are hereby established as such to achieve a village                            -
  25| style design for the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District.
  26| (1) Occupied lot area.
  27| (a) The total area on any lot devoted to building, parking, outdoor storage, and display and other hard-
  28| surfaced areas may occupy up to 85% of the total lot area.
  29| (2) Maximum height: no more than 55 feet measured from ground level.
  30| (3) Setbacks. A landscape plan shall be required for screening and buffering purposes for setback are-
  31| as.
  32|      Front: 20 feet.
  33|      Side: 15 feet.
  34|      Rear: 20 feet.
  35| (4) Minimum lot size: 20,000 square feet.
  36| (5) Minimum frontage: 50 feet.
  37| (6)  Minimum open space. All projects within the Summer Street Revitalization Overlay District shall
  38|      have at least 10% of the total site area devoted to open space; required setbacks shall be consid-
  39|      ered as part of the total area required for open space. The required open space shall not be used for
  40|      parking or loading purposes and shall be open and unobstructed to the sky. Items such as benches,
  41|      walkways, planters, landscaping, kiosks, gazebos and similar structures shall not be considered
  42|      obstructions.
  43| (7)  Mixed-use developments that propose to have retail and residential uses within the same building
  44|      on lots that meet the minimum dimensional requirements established herein shall be allowed only
  45|      in a two-story building.
  46|                                                                 208
